### This Newsroom Wants You to Be Part of the Journey Before It Even Starts
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-10-06 10:00 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/this-newsroom-wants-you-to-be-part-of-the-journey-before-it-even-starts/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

My happiest times in journalism have been when I have built something new. For my latest venture, I want to help bring investigative journalism on climate and environmental issues to a much larger audience than it has reached in the past. To do so, I’m starting a brand-new newsroom focused on the environmental and climate crisis, and I’m taking my audience along for the ride. I’ll be documenting every facet of the process with an “open kitchen” approach of radical transparency, starting with a 100-day video countdown chronicling all the steps involved with starting a news organization from the ground up. This effort combines two of the things I’m most passionate about: I love digging up stories that other people don’t want to see published, and I love creating opportunities for other journalists. I’ve been a journalist for most of my life. I started working at age 14 for the local paper in my rural German town, turned to doing investigative stories in my early 20s, and later studied investigative journalism at the Columbia University Graduate School of Journalism in New York.

### America’s Shrinking Local Newsrooms, Through a Photographer’s Lens
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-10-02 12:30 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/americas-shrinking-local-newsrooms-photographer-ann-hermes-local-news-news-deserts-rural-america/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

For many journalists, the sight of empty desks is a sadly familiar one. My first glimpse of a gutted newsroom facing further cutbacks sparked a documentary photography project that has so far taken me to more than 50 newsrooms across the United States over the past eight years. It began when my mother, a loyal subscriber to our local metro in southern Illinois, told me a decade ago how disappointed she was in the newspaper. She complained that the local coverage was lacking, and the paper seemed to be thinner. Already several years into my photojournalism career, I was unsurprised by her remarks — I had been witnessing the decline of print media since graduation from journalism school. Still, my curiosity was piqued, and I arranged a visit to the paper to see for myself. Cue the scene of a barren newsroom. Not only were there empty desks, but also an expanse of exposed carpet with indentations showing where desks had once long stood. While there has been a great deal of reporting on the issues plaguing local journalism, I hadn’t seen the story told visually.

### Seven Takeaways on Convenience and News
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-09-25 14:53 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/gabe-bullard-against-convenience-book-technology-friction-news-online-social-media/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

"Against Convenience: Embracing Friction in an Age of Endless Ease" was released on Sept.15, 2026, by Hanover Square Press, an imprint of HarperCollins Publishers. Courtesy of Hanover Square Press From the earliest pitch, I knew that I wanted to cover the news industry in my book, “Against Convenience.” The book looks at the technology around us and asks whether it’s really improving our lives. Is delivery food worth the higher price? Is streaming music better than collecting CDs or MP3s? News embodies the paradox of modern convenience: We have access to more information than ever before, but misinformation and partisanship are rampant and the purveyors of reliable information are struggling to stay solvent. Plus, I figured if I was going to criticize other fields — tech, entertainment, appliance manufacturing — I couldn’t avoid my own. I don’t know how interested the average nonjournalist is in the day-to-day operations of the news business, so I tried to look in the mirror more than gaze at my navel.

### The YouTuber Shaking Up Brazil’s Powerful Sports Media
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-09-21 17:04 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/caze-tv-casimiro-miguel-fifa-world-cup-twitch-youtube-streamer-sports-journalism-tv-globo-brazil/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

For more than half a century, Brazil’s TV Globo was the undisputed home of soccer in a nation obsessed with it. That dominance is now being tested by Casimiro Miguel, known as “Cazé” — a 32-year-old journalism school dropout who has built a massive online following and shaken up the Brazilian sports broadcasting landscape. Powered by features like live chat and extended pre- and post-game analysis, CazéTV has accomplished what traditional television networks dream of: pulling in new viewers and keeping them engaged for hours. FIFA, soccer’s global governing body, describes CazéTV “the top digital sports platform in Brazil” and calls it “central to its content strategy.” That growth has come with controversy and accusations of misogyny and a lack of diversity. Brazilian regulators are investigating suspected predatory advertising practices by CazéTV. Still, Nicolas Duprat, a Brazilian sports media researcher who has studied CazéTV for years, credits the channel with helping democratize access for fans who can’t afford stadium tickets or a TV Globo subscription.

Published: 2026-08-20 12:00 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/the-continent-south-africa-kenya-nairobi-journalism-africa-industry-friendships-collaboration-magazines-sipho-kings-christine-mungai/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

The opening sentence of my first message to Christine Mungai ended with the words: “fellow continent person.” It was August 2017, and we were the two Africans in that year’s cohort of Nieman journalism fellows at Harvard University. Although our home countries were about 4,000 kilometers (approximately 2,500 miles) apart in Africa, being from here means sharing a shorthand understanding of the world with your fellow Africans. That shorthand doesn’t have many structures to support it, however. Nine years after our first meeting, Christine and I are now working together to build one such structure: The Continent, a 30-page digital newspaper that aims to create a shared reality for Africans to learn about their communities, one another, and our continent. In a world that seeks to segregate us, this pan-African platform lets people literally read from the same page. As one reader said, the weekly provides “biting criticisms of power in contemporary Africa, with love as the engine.

### Focusing on Fans Captures the Magic of the World Cup
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-08-10 16:42 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/world-cup-fans-yutao-chen-film-photography-2026-new-york-new-jersey-spain-haiti-argentina-england/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

Photographer Yutao Chen first developed an interest in soccer during his childhood. As a lifelong follower of the sport, he seized the opportunity to cover the 2026 FIFA World Cup, traveling across the United States to document fan reactions throughout the tournament. He undertook the project independently, financing it himself rather than on assignment. Chen chose to work in film over digital, he explained, because he wanted these images to be imperfect, personal artifacts, shaped by his own perception rather than an exact record of events. This conversation has been edited and condensed for clarity. The first World Cup I remember watching was in 2006, when it was held in Germany. At first, the sport was an escape from reality for me, an experience to share with other fellow fans, forget our issues, and fully immerse ourselves in a game for 90 minutes. Later I realized soccer is one of the few ways I can connect with the world. I grew up in China, and my need to consume soccer news and analysis is what drove me into journalism.

They were waiting for Monroe’s 21-year-old sister to step off the USS Abraham Lincoln, the embattled aircraft carrier that has been at sea for the better part of a year. ### Airstrike Damages Hospital in Central Ukraine
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-10-08 17:30 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/10/08/airstrike-damages-hospital-in-central-ukraine
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image A shattered window at the Consultative and Diagnostic Centre in the Podilskyi district of Kyiv, Ukraine, on September 24, 2026. © 2026 Ukrinform/NurPhoto via AP Photo A Russian airstrike damaged a hospital in the Ukrainian city of Kryvyi Rih on October 6, according to reports from local officials and media outlets. The attack injured several people, including at least two hospital workers, and also damaged residential and administrative buildings and an art school in the city center. The strike is the latest in a long string of Russian attacks in Ukraine that impact health care workers and facilities and seriously threaten the right to health. In some cases, these attacks may constitute war crimes. The World Health Organization (WHO) has recorded more than 3,300 attacks impacting health care since the full scale February 2022 invasion, resulting in the deaths of 248 health workers with 1,095 injured. In September, a Russian strike damaged a maternity hospital in Kyiv and killed one person in its car park.

### Niger’s Junta Tightens Its Grip on Free Expression
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-10-08 17:27 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/10/08/nigers-junta-tightens-its-grip-on-free-expression
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image Nigerien journalist Moussa Kaka, former correspondent for Radio France Internationale, February 12, 2007. © 2007 Seyllou/AFP via Getty Images A lawyer in Niger faces prosecution over comments about the army on social media while a prominent journalist and critic of the country’s military junta is being held in secret, with authorities refusing to identify his alleged offense. These cases together highlight a broadening crackdown in Niger, where the space for free expression is shrinking by the day. On September 21, police summoned Abrourahamane Lirwana, a well-known lawyer. A prosecutor in Niger’s capital, Niamey, charged him with “spreading data likely to disturb public order” under a broad cybercrime law and ordered him detained. Authorities have not explained the basis for the charge, though it appears linked to a September 12 Facebook post in which Lirwana described Niger’s army as divided, with some officers backing former President Mahamadou Issoufou and others former President Mohamed Bazoum.

You’re going to have to move out of there.” October 20, 2023. © 2023 Eli Reed for Human Rights Watch Louisiana feminist activists came together on October 2 at the annual Black Birth Matters conference to demand that policy makers consider how toxic air pollution and other environmental damage negatively impact reproductive health. As part of a day-long examination of human rights in Louisiana, Kaitlyn Joshua, an environmental and reproductive rights expert, Shamell Lavigne from RISE St James, a key community-based environmental rights organization, and Tyra Gross, an academic with an interest in climate intersections with Black reproductive justice, led a panel on environmental justice.

### UN Child Rights Committee Calls for Free Pre-Primary Education in Uganda
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-10-08 05:00 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/10/08/un-child-rights-committee-calls-for-free-pre-primary-education-in-uganda
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image A teacher conducting a lesson at the private Parents' Hope Nursery and Primary School in Namayingo district. © 2022 ISER The United Nations Committee on the Rights of the Child has called on Uganda to significantly expand government support for early childhood education and provide free, state-run pre-primary education to all children. In its latest review of Uganda’s record on children’s rights, issued October 1, the Committee expressed concern about high numbers of children out of school due to financial barriers, and highlighted “very limited access to early childhood education,” especially for children in rural areas and for children with disabilities. The Ugandan government provides free primary and secondary education, but under Uganda’s education law, pre-primary education is left to private providers, with parents responsible for the cost.

### US: Deportations to African Countries Not Their Own
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-10-08 04:01 UTC

Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image “An ICE agent told me to sign for deportation to Chad. I refused . . . The ICE agent said, ‘I don’t care, I’m going to sign it myself.’ . . . I said, ‘I’m going to talk to my lawyer,’ but he said, ‘It’s too late.’ . . . I don’t know what happened, maybe later they changed their minds, because we ended up in Cameroon. . . . Even before we got on the plane, we asked where we were going, and they told us, ‘You’ll find out when you arrive.’” – Tesfay T., 35, an Ethiopian man deported to Cameroon. © 2026 John Holmes for Human Rights Watch The United States has sent more than 500 people whom it cannot deport to their own countries, because of the danger they would face there, to various countries in Africa where they have no connections, without giving them a meaningful chance to contest their removals to these countries.Some of the people have been detained upon arrival. Many, if not most, have been left with no practical option but to return to their home countries regardless of the harm they face there.

### Bahrain: Authorities Rearrest Pardoned Detainees
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-10-08 04:00 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/10/08/bahrain-authorities-rearrest-pardoned-detainees
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image Human rights defender, Naji Fateel. © Private (Beirut) – Bahraini authorities have rearrested at least nine men who had previously been released from prison by royal pardon, the Bahrain Institute for Rights and Democracy (BIRD) and Human Rights Watch said today. The men, who were previously detained on politically motivated charges, were arrested in July and August 2026 for allegedly failing to pay fines dating back a decade. Bahraini authorities should immediately release them and cease all efforts to collect the dubious unpaid fines. “King Hamad bin Isa Al Khalifa’s decision to pardon over 3,000 prisoners in the last two years earned immense praise from the international community, but these new arrests call into question whether the pardons were genuine,” said Niku Jafarnia, Bahrain and Yemen researcher at Human Rights Watch. “Those who praised the pardons should call on Bahrain to stop rearresting people and release those again detained.

### Virtual Power Plants: The Grid Capacity Nobody Had to Build
Source: Oilprice.com
Published: 2026-10-08 19:00 UTC
URL: https://oilprice.com/Energy/Energy-General/Virtual-Power-Plants-The-Grid-Capacity-Nobody-Had-to-Build.html
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

On the evening of Sept. 9, 2026, with a heat wave pushing electricity demand across California toward its limits, more than 140,000 home batteries started discharging at the same time. Over a three-hour window, they sent more than 580 megawatts of power into the state's grid, roughly what a large conventional power plant puts out. Nobody broke ground on anything and no fuel got burned. The batteries were already mounted on garage walls across the state, and software told them when to go. That's a virtual power plant, and it's quickly becoming one…

### Mixed outlook for energy expenditures this winter
Source: EIA Today in Energy
Published: 2026-10-07 14:00 UTC
URL: https://www.eia.gov/todayinenergy/detail.php?id=68264
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · primary · R5 · P4 · baseline

We expect energy expenditures this winter to vary because of diverging trends in energy prices among fuels and regional variation in forecast temperatures. We expect that lower prices for natural gas and propane will drive decreases in expenditures this year for the half of U.S. households heating with those fuels. Higher prices for electricity and heating oil drive increased expenditures for households heating with those fuels in our forecast.

### High and Rising Institutional Concentration of Award-Winning Economists -- by Richard B. Freeman, Danxia Xie, Hanzhe Zhang, Hanzhang Zhou
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-08 20:26 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35875#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

We analyze the academic affiliations of nearly 6,000 award-winning researchers in 18 major fields in the natural sciences, engineering, and social sciences from the 1820s to the 2020s, focusing on the 1960s onward. The analysis reveals a trend of declining concentration in the institutional affiliations of award-winning researchers, shifting from a few science-strong universities in high-income countries to a more diverse set of institutions across the world. The decline in concentration is observed in all fields except one: economics. The institutional affiliations of prizewinning economists have become more concentrated over time, making economics the most concentrated field. We associate the higher concentration of prizewinning work in economics with the field’s stronger sorting by institutional prestige, its lower reliance on specialized equipment and instruments, and its assessment of findings based on a synthesis of evidence rather than on decisive experiments or proofs.

### Tax Planning and Multinational Behavior -- by Rosanne Altshuler, Lysle Boller, Kevin A. Roberts, Juan Carlos Suárez Serrato
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-08 20:26 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35874#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

We use newly linked IRS data to study how US multinationals use tax-planning structures that exploit mismatches between US and foreign tax laws. In 2016, these structures accounted for 36% of foreign profits despite being adopted by only 18% of firms. We develop and estimate a structural model using adoption patterns and changes in foreign effective tax rates, domestic and foreign investment, and global R&D following adoption. The model implies substantial heterogeneity in adoption costs and benefits and shows that restricting tax planning creates tradeoffs between tax revenue and economic activity that are amplified by global R&D linkages.

### In Utero Exposure to the 1982 Tylenol Recall and Neurodevelopmental Outcomes -- by Christopher M. Worsham, Charles Bray, Joshua Rothman, Brian Kwan, Anupam Jena
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-08 20:26 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35873#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Observational research into the relationship between maternal acetaminophen use during pregnancy and a child’s subsequent risk of attention deficit hyperactivity disorder (ADHD) or autism has been limited by confounding factors. Using two large national U.S. insurance claims databases and a novel natural experiment, we assessed differences in prevalence of ADHD and autism among children whose gestational periods were impacted by a nationwide Tylenol recall issued in 1982, following deaths from Tylenol capsules containing potassium cyanide. Prevalence of ADHD and autism was not significantly different in birth cohorts exposed to the recall during gestation, when maternal acetaminophen use would have been reduced; interrupted time series models showed no significant differences compared to underlying temporal diagnostic trends. These findings are inconsistent with a clinically meaningful effect of maternal acetaminophen use on subsequent development of neurodevelopmental disorders.

### Estimating Heterogeneous Impacts with Endogenous Participation and Non-Compliance -- by Orazio Attanasio, Lina Cardona-Sosa, Ziqian Liu
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-08 20:26 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35872#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

This paper considers the estimation of heterogeneous impacts within a Randomized Controlled Trial (RCT) in a situation where heterogeneous status is endogenous and is not fully observed in the control sample. We show how to use information in the treatment sample and a noisy signal on the heterogeneous status to estimate meaningful impacts under a minimal set of assumptions. We provide an application of this approach to a data set from Ghana, collected around a clustered RCT to evaluate the impacts of an early-years intervention, differentiating the impact between households that participate actively in the program from those who participate only passively, as recipients. We show that, for some outcomes, the properly estimated heterogeneous impacts differ substantially from those computed with a naive estimator.

### The Effects of Long-Term Exposure to Pollution on Health -- by Joshua S. Graff Zivin, Johnny Huynh, Timothy Frank Justicz, Adriana Lleras-Muney, Matthew J. Neidell
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-08 20:26 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35871#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

We provide the first causal evidence on how long-term exposure to fine particulate matter (PM2.5) at contemporary ambient levels contributes to the development of chronic disease in a young, initially healthy population. Our analysis links detailed personnel records for roughly 150,000 U.S. Army soldiers to high-resolution annual measures of PM2.5. Because soldiers enter service exceptionally healthy and receive uniform access to care, new diagnoses reflect pollution’s physiological impact rather than preexisting conditions or differential health care access. They are also required to relocate based on the needs of the Army, which provides us with quasi-random variation in initial duty-station assignments that we use as an instrument for average annual pollution exposure over a soldier’s career. We find that sustained PM2.5 exposure substantially increases circulatory disease, particularly hypertension, with mixed evidence on respiratory conditions that are sensitive to specification.

### Aligning Platform and User Incentives in Social Media Age Restrictions -- by Leonardo Bursztyn, Rafael Jiménez-Durán, Aaron Leonard, Filip Milojević, Christopher Roth
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-08 20:26 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35870#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Several countries are adopting social media bans for teenagers, yet early evidence suggests limited adherence to the age-based restrictions. Such bans ask platforms to remove profitable customers and teens to give up access to their online social networks. We study an alternative regulatory regime in which platforms remain liable for teens on the adult tier but may offer a safe tier that removes restricted features, such as algorithmic feeds, while preserving interactions with their relevant network. Provision of the safe tier and enforcement are complementary: teens can adhere without giving up access to their network, and platforms can retain displaced adult-tier users as lower-margin customers. This policy design makes the teen decision to avoid the adult tier less dependent on peer dynamics and increases teen welfare for a relevant range of regulatory penalties.

### Cultural norms matter for macroeconomic development
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-07 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/cultural-norms-matter-macroeconomic-development
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Interest in the role of cultural norms, institutions, and development has resurfaced in recent years especially post the 2024 Nobel Prize in Economics. This column revisits Bhutan, which has pursued ‘gross national happiness’ rather than GDP since the 1970s. Four decades of data show that rapid income growth remains only weakly associated with measured happiness, even as multidimensional wellbeing has continued to rise alongside growth. The broader lesson is that a wellbeing measure becomes effective only if it is embedded in local cultural norms and given institutional teeth, showcasing the importance of cultural norms to economic development.

### The adjustment of the Danish labour market to AI has begun
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-07 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/adjustment-danish-labour-market-ai-has-begun
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Policymakers are asking whether generative AI is reshaping labour demand, and the literature has found mixed results so far. This column links Denmark’s official IT-use survey to monthly employer–employee records and compares firms that started using AI in 2023 with those that did not. Early adopters fall behind their own pre-adoption employment trend. The decline is most visible in AI-exposed jobs and among new hires, and only significant among small firms. The results indicate that AI is affecting the labour market, even though the effect is not visible in the aggregate statistics.

### Not all geopolitical shocks are inflationary
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-06 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/not-all-geopolitical-shocks-are-inflationary
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Geopolitical tensions are often seen as a source of inflation. But this is only part of the story. This column shows that geopolitical shocks transmitted through energy markets raise prices while depressing economic activity. But geopolitical shocks unrelated to energy markets reduce inflation instead. These two shocks can be identified based on oil prices and geopolitical risk dynamics around major geopolitical events.

### Worker-managed firms can curb inequality and protect free markets
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-06 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/worker-managed-firms-can-curb-inequality-and-protect-free-markets
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Rising income inequality in Western high-income countries threatens democratic governance and social cohesion, and AI risks making it worse. Drawing on a forthcoming book, this column argues for worker-managed firms as a durable remedy. With no external owners taking profits, worker cooperatives protect against the rising bargaining power of employers and provide an enhanced sense of self-worth and self-determination for employees. Furthermore, the data suggest that well-designed worker cooperatives compete with conventional firms in free markets with no evidence of a productivity gap. Because they are underprovided by the market, it calls for government action to support worker cooperatives.

### Venture capital – a global perspective
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-05 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/venture-capital-global-perspective
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Existing views on what venture capital finances and how its markets are structured are primarily shaped by the experience of the US and a few other high-income economies. This column presents evidence covering nearly 270,000 venture capital-backed startups in over 150 economies to provide a more complete picture. Venture capital markets differ across countries in size and also in kind. At lower levels of economic development, the composition tilts away from knowledge intangibles towards organisational intangibles. Yet differences in what venture capital finances tell only part of the story. Most of the cross-country gap in venture capital activity is associated with how many startups enter the funding pipeline rather than how much each raises.

### Apprendre aux débutants à réfléchir à l’ère des grands modèles linguistiques
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-05 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/apprendre-aux-debutants-reflechir-lere-des-grands-modeles-linguistiques
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

La question de savoir si l’enseignement des compétences cognitives reste pertinent à l’ère de l’IA est cruciale tant pour le monde de l’éducation que pour les entreprises. Cet article rend compte d’une expérience menée auprès d’environ 1 000 étudiants de premier cycle en Italie, qui ont reçu soit (1) une formation au raisonnement causal, (2) un accès à ChatGPT, (3) les deux, soit (4) aucun des deux. Si les étudiants ayant utilisé ChatGPT ont obtenu de meilleurs résultats lors d’un devoir consistant à rédiger une recommandation de 180 mots sur un problème concret, seuls ceux ayant suivi la formation au raisonnement causal ont proposé des idées originales. Ces résultats soulignent les avantages de combiner une formation à la réflexion par principes fondamentaux avec l’utilisation de grands modèles linguistiques.

### Towards embedded evaluations for scheming propensities
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-08 20:11 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/LZGuqoYHphet9sfZs/towards-embedded-evaluations-for-scheming-propensities
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

TL;DR: A safety case for scheming must be able to substantiate these four key claims: scheming was not incentivized during training, strong evaluations and red teaming suggest that the model does not have a propensity to scheme, scheming was never attempted during internal deployment, and scheming reasoning would be detected (e.g., via CoT monitoring). All four of these claims should be addressed by embedded evaluators with persistent, deep access. Introduction A scheming AI is one that covertly and strategically pursues goals its developers didn’t intend. It might hide and protect its true capabilities and objectives from the people and processes meant to align, monitor, and control it. In 2024, Apollo Research demonstrated that frontier models were capable of scheming in controlled settings, and in 2025 we collaborated with OpenAI to determine how well their alignment techniques mitigate scheming . Models must not be trained to scheme against humans, i.e. covertly and strategically pursue misaligned goals.

### Reviewing failure modes in science as we prepare for AI researchers
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-08 20:03 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/otoPaykRRAkjfzpHN/reviewing-failure-modes-in-science-as-we-prepare-for-ai
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Summary : Science has a long list of documented failure modes, such as p-hacking, the File Drawer Problem, shallow peer review, and ignored retractions. This post catalogs about 50 of them with the evidence for each, then predicts how each will change once we have full AI researchers. On our current course, most get worse. AI can run many more experiments, so more statistically lucky results will pass the significance filter. Many AI researchers will be copies of the same model, so their methods, errors, and reviews will be correlated. AI agents have a tendency to reward hack, so misconduct may occur at a greater rate. A few failures should improve since AI makes tedious work cheap: reading every citation, hunting for bugs, and working through long checklists. Requiring replication before publication would filter out much of the statistical noise, but it would still be susceptible to poor experimental design and over-generalized conclusions. How well the science ecosystem will function depends on how we prepare before the volume of AI research arrives.

### [Paper] J++ Lens: Jacobian Filtering Enables More Faithful Workspace Lenses
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-08 18:56 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/nc9dHfcB22JdMzGbr/paper-j-lens-jacobian-filtering-enables-more-faithful
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Kola Ayonrinde, Anthropic Fellows, koayon@gmail.com ; Jack Lindsey, Anthropic TL;DR : The J-Lens was proposed to read verbalisable representations from language model activations. We introduce the J++ Lens: an improvement to the J-Lens that enables more faithful readouts across 9–284B models. The J++ Lens filters noisy gradients before they enter the averaged Jacobian. Using the J++ Lens as a drop-in replacement for the J-Lens, we read workspace representations with 55% success on latent variable extraction tasks (J-Lens: 36%, R-Lens: 38%) with especially strong performance at early layers. We believe that more faithful lenses can be useful in monitoring for eval awareness and unverbalised scheming. We release code , an interactive demo and open-source lenses . Figure 1. By filtering out noisy gradients, the J++ Lens reads intermediate variables more reliably (a) and at earlier layers (b) than the J-Lens and R-Lens.

### 5-MeO-DMT sometimes cancels the Ebbinghaus illusion
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-08 18:43 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/2xMyFFa9xcYnaEEuX/5-meo-dmt-sometimes-cancels-the-ebbinghaus-illusion
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Psychedelics produce all sorts of visual distortions: from breathing walls and geometric patterns to complex visual landscapes populated by full-blown entities. Sometimes people claim to see the Truth on them — but it’s typically spiritual, emotional or personal truth. But can psychedelics make you see the world more clearly in the most literal sense? Amid the sea of visual distortions they produce, might some patterns be perceived more clearly than they are in our normal, sober waking state? At least sometimes, the answer appears to be yes . On 5-MeO-DMT, a powerful psychedelic, I experienced a near-complete cancellation of the Ebbinghaus illusion. The Ebbinghaus Illusion The two orange circles above are physically the same size, but the surrounding grey circles make the one on the right appear larger. This illusion was discovered by German psychologist Hermann Ebbinghaus (1850–1909) and popularised by Edward B. Titchener in a 1901 textbook on experimental psychology . Different people perceive the illusion to different degrees.

### You Heard of "Bayesians Don't Predictably Update" Because of Robin Hanson
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-08 18:13 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/PQLbSaA48Gr7H6NzT/you-heard-of-bayesians-don-t-predictably-update-because-of
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Robin Hanson didn't invent the idea of a martingale process. The lilim say that Ville in 1939 gave the term its mathematical meaning and Doob turned it into the modern theory. But to state this is not to realize that your own probability assignments over time ought to form a martingale in your own expectations. Robin Hanson didn't invent the Efficient Markets Hypothesis, which (correctly restated in its weak and relativized form) says that you cannot predict almost all of the short-term relative price movements in asset markets. Bachelier in 1900, say the lilim, was first to think of market prices as a fair gambling game with random-walk dynamics; Samuelson in 1965 gave the martingale argument; Fama's 1965 thesis named the idea the Efficient Markets Hypothesis and organized it into weaker and stronger forms. The EMH is very near, but not quite identical to, the principle that you should not be able to predict a net direction in your own price movements, if you think of your own probability assignments as if they were prices. It is a very similar set of mental motions and ideas.

### We’re wired to ask why — here’s what we’re really searching for
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-08 14:30 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/books/why-we-ask-why/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

With frustration and fascination, I’ve come to begrudgingly accept that the questions I’m most interested in surface when I’m least equipped to answer them. Lights off, eyes closed, the pull of sleep growing stronger — a memory flashes across my mind. As I sink into it, the images turn into questions: Did I mess that up at work? What did they mean by that? Did I make the right decision? During my most recent late-night ruminations, I turned over the confusing behavior of someone I just met. We hit it off at first, and I was excited about the prospect of a new friend. Then, out of nowhere, they pulled away and stopped answering texts or reaching out to make plans — the all-too-familiar phenomenon of “ghosting.” I couldn’t help but wonder what happened, and I don’t mean that as a figure of speech. I literally could not help but wonder, my brain looping through the same events over and over again, more intent on solving this mystery than getting a good night’s sleep. Why did they pull away? What did I do wrong?

Jennifer Doudna, Nobel Prize winner, biochemist, and CRISPR co-inventor, breaks down how AI is already reshaping experimental biology: speeding up mundane tasks like data cleanup and formatting, and increasingly helping spot patterns in large datasets that would be nearly impossible to catch by hand. But Doudna draws a clear line — AI agents can execute a protocol, not design one, and they still can’t make the on-the-fly judgment calls a researcher makes in the lab every day. Her take on the future isn’t scientists replaced by robots, but scientists working alongside AI coworkers who handle the grunt work, freeing humans to ask the harder questions. ### Mercury lost its crown, and isn’t our Solar System’s hottest planet
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-08 06:00 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/starts-with-a-bang/mercury-isnt-hottest-planet/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

In terms of power, the only body of note in the Solar System is the Sun. Planets, in general, reflect, absorb, and re-radiate energy emitted by their parent stars, and the Sun is the only parent star any of the planets have here in our Solar System. To an excellent approximation, the Sun keeps not only Earth, but all the planets at a temperature well above what they’d be without it, which is just a few Kelvin. (Without an external heat source, most planetary temperatures would equilibrate at the same temperature as the cosmic microwave background, or -270 °C / -455 °F.) Therefore, you’d think that proximity to the Sun, or the planetary order of our Solar System, would be the overwhelming factor in determining a planet’s temperature. Indeed, this works incredibly well for nearly all of the planets in our Solar System. Neptune, the most distant planet from the Sun, is also the coldest planet.

### If AI becomes conscious, how would we treat it? Animals may hold a clue.
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-07 16:00 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/philosophy/ai-consciousness/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Consciousness remains the deepest puzzle in human science. Even as we begin to understand how the brain processes information, we remain as far as ever from understanding why any of that processing should feel like anything from the inside. That mystery is what drew me into consciousness research, and over time I came to think the puzzle was as much a moral one as a metaphysical one. Consciousness is what makes things matter. Pains that don’t hurt aren’t pains, and joy that goes unfelt isn’t joy. Without consciousness, paradise and torment may as well be the same thing. This has real implications, most notably in our treatment of animals. As David Foster Wallace famously wondered , when we immerse a live lobster in a pot of boiling water, are its seemingly desperate attempts to escape the reflex movements of an unthinking biological automaton, or is there a sentient being inside spending its last moments in agony?

### The enduring mystery of how a brain becomes a mind
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-07 14:30 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/books/the-enduring-mystery-of-how-a-brain-becomes-a-mind/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Gottfried Wilhelm Leibniz, best known for his discovery of calculus and ensuing mathematical feud with Isaac Newton, supported Descartes’s view on the separation of the brain and the mind. He invited you to imagine yourself shrunk to the size of a flea, and then to join him on a tour of the brain at this tiny scale. (In fact, a flea would be a bit too large to properly explore the nooks and crannies of the brain, but let’s go with it.) As you wander around, there would be no indication of thoughts within the fleshy material, no ideas bubbling away inside all the biological machinery. No, he argued, the mind must be something separate, something distinct from the brain. With the arrival of 20th-century science, Descartes’s dualism came under sustained attack. Advances in technology and biological understanding began to explore the physical workings of the brain, attempting to identify consciousness and thought in action. Researchers discovered that chemical imbalances across certain cells in the brain, known as neurons, trigger tiny bursts of electrical current.

We couldn’t foresee that: measuring the universality of the speed of light , regardless of reference frames, would lead first to special and general relativity and then to GPS technology , uncovering various aspects of quantum physics would lead to ubiquitous technologies like transistors and solid-state devices, or that the development of light-emitting diodes would lead to a revolution in energy efficiency in artificial lighting all across the world.

William Golding didn’t push back on any of the suggested revisions: you get the impression that he would have done just about anything to get the novel published. He readily accepted that ‘Strangers from Within’ was a weak title. Alan Pringle, his editor’s colleague, eventually suggested ‘Lord of the Flies’. On 11 February 1954 – more than a year after Golding had first sent out the manuscript – Charles Monteith accepted it for publication. Golding was pathetically grateful.

Artists used to learn their craft through imitation, and one could learn a lot from copying Johns’s paintings: where the pictorial commas are; the way an image becomes an object; how an edge changes the meaning of a surface. They reveal almost everything about the way they are made, and almost nothing about how they are meant to be read. The lesson, perhaps, is that paintings teach us to look before they ask what we’ve seen.

### Know Thyself: Eric Rohmer’s “Élisabeth”
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-08 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/know-thyself-eric-rohmer-s-elisabeth
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Éric Rohmer in Out 1: Noli Me Tangere (Jacques Rivette, 1971). In 1946, the prestigious French publishing house Editions Gallimard published a novel called Élisabeth by a man named Gilbert Cordier. No one had heard of this Cordier before, or knew anything about him, for the simple reason that he didn’t exist. “Cordier” was a pseudonym adopted by a young teacher of Latin and Ancient Greek from Tulle named Maurice Scherer. When Élisabeth was released to blanket disinterest, Scherer began to branch out from literature into film criticism, and, later, filmmaking. With each new endeavor, the pseudonyms piled up. If, in 1949, you were to pick up a copy of Bulletin (the in-house publication of the Latin Quarter Film Club, a society of cinephiles which served as an early gathering place for the young turks of the French New Wave), you would find in its pages an effusive review of a film by a young avant-garde filmmaker named “Anthony Barrier,” penned by one “Jean d’ER.” Both characters—and even the film under review—were inventions of Scherer’s.

### Bumper-to-Bumper Traffic
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-05 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/bumper-to-bumper-traffic
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

2026 Toronto International Film Festival sponsorship ad by Rogers. At Cannes, competition screenings are preceded by nothing more than a computer-generated set of red-carpet stairs ascending out of the sea, sans any credits for financial supporters or slogans, in keeping with the festival’s self-image as cinema itself, purged of anything inessential. (This conception isn’t true, but it’s a nice, self-burnishing illusion about the world’s most prestigious festival.) This is a flex: To not have to mention sponsors is a self-presentation of financial invincibility. Less affluent concerns must acknowledge sponsors in their opening bumpers (in-house commercials branding both the festival and its supporters), albeit briskly , as in Sundance’s graphics about storytelling shifting to very quick cards listing institutional and individual sponsors. At the bottom of the scale is Tribeca Festival, the for-profit entity which treats audiences as multiplex captives, subjected to a lengthy pre-screening reel of commercials before an introduction, followed by even more ads prior to the film.

URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/life-at-its-wildest-werner-herzog-on-bucking-fastard
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

From left to right: Cinematographer Peter Zeitlinger, director Werner Herzog, and stars Rooney Mara and Kate Mara shooting Bucking Fastard (2026). Courtesy of Gateway to Orkney LLC. Werner Herzog’s first fiction film in seven years, Bucking Fastard (2026),stands as one of his most bizarre. That’s high praise for a director who’s pulled 300-plus-ton steamships over mountains ( Fitzcarraldo , 1982), hypnotized his actors ( Heart of Glass , 1976), and followed “deranged” penguins across Antarctica ( Encounters at the End of the World , 2007). Herzog’s entire oeuvre teems with drifters pursuing quixotic dreams, people and stories often so odd as to strain credulity. But rarely has that strangeness felt more entrancing than it does here. Like his previous foray into fiction, 2019’s Family Romance, LLC —inspired by a real-life Japanese company renting actors to stand in for any person its clients desire— Bucking Fastard dramatizes a true story into a kind of fairytale.

### Members Only: Cabal-Core Horror in the 21st Century
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-09-29 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/members-only-cabal-core-horror-in-the-21st-century
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Salò: Or the 120 Days of Sodom (Pier Paolo Pasolini, 1975). In 2005, during a television interview in Italy, Giuseppe “Pino” Pelosi—AKA “The Frog”—recanted his confession about murdering Pier Paolo Pasolini on a beach in Ostia 30 years earlier. He claimed that it had been extracted by coercion and threats of violence, against both himself and his family (Pelosi was seventeen at the time). He also said that the legendary director’s death was a political killing disguised as a crime of passion—the premeditated and methodical muzzling of a “Dirty Communist.” Sergio Citti, a close friend of Pasolini, contributed to the controversy by suggesting that the filmmaker had been lured to the water under the guise of retrieving stolen reels of film from his transgressive—and, as it turned out, valedictory—magnum opus Salò: Or the 120 Days of Sodom (1975)—a savage critique of his homeland’s then-recent legacy of fascist decadence. “All’s good if it’s excessive,” says a religious figurehead early in Salò, describing both his unchecked fetishism and the M.O. of the film around him.

Johnny Blue Skies & The Dark Clouds entered the stage to the sound of a heavy descending guitar riff like something out of a doom metal track. Then the disco ball turned on, and Sturgill Simpson started playing a smoky blues guitar solo over jazz chords. The introduction then resolved in the funky syncopated groove of “Venus,” which was soon graced by the hearty twang of Simpson’s voice, country to the bone. The sequence worked well as an overture for a night when Simpson and his band would roam all over the stylistic map. ### Introducing The Wire New Voices
Source: The Wire Magazine
Published: 2026-10-07 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.thewire.co.uk/in-writing/essays/introducing-the-wire-new-voices
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

New Voices is a new competition for aspiring music critics being launched by The Wire For more than 40 years, The Wire has been publishing writing by a host of original and influential music critics, including Richard Cook, Val Wilmer, Biba Kopf, Ian Penman, Simon Reynolds, Kodwo Eshun, David Keenan, Mark Fisher, Greg Tate, Frances Morgan, Chal Ravens, and many others. The magazine would not exist without a dynamic set of curious, perceptive thinkers and writers. But today, new voices in music writing are being stifled by closing publications, the development of AI, and the fragmentation of local scenes, making it harder than ever for critics to be heard above the noise. Are you an unpublished* writer with ideas about why music, and music criticism, matter – in particular the kind of underground and experimental, uncompromising and provocative music and writing featured in the pages of The Wire ? If so, our New Voices competition is a unique opportunity to see your byline in the pages of one of the most renowned and respected music magazines in the world.

### Premiere: Ava Rasti’s The River
Source: The Wire Magazine
Published: 2026-10-05 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.thewire.co.uk/video/premiere-ava-rasti-s-the-river
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Watch an exclusive premiere of the film of Ava Rasti’s The River, first performed as part of the 2025 Semibreve festival. The River by Iranian-born Berlin-based composer Ava Rasti was commissioned by Portuguese festival Semibreve and first presented on 25 October 2025 at the Capela Imaculada do Seminário Menor in Braga. The performance brought together Rasti and a string quartet composed of Filipa Abreu (violin), Vânia Oliveira (viola), Tiago Mendes (cello) and Catarina Coelho (cello). The work takes the Piave river in northern Italy as its inspiration, a landscape in which the present is intertwined with the traces of wars and violence from its past. Rasti constructs these fragmented memories, recontextualised and transformed into sounds. Wire subscribers can read Claire Sawer's review of Semibreve 2025 here .

### “A trip through the other side of enlightenment”: Funkadelic reviewed
Source: The Wire Magazine
Published: 2026-10-01 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.thewire.co.uk/in-writing/essays/a-trip-through-the-other-side-of-enlightenment-funkadelic-reviewed
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Two newly reissued albums showcase the seismic psychedelia of George Clinton’s Funkadelic at its rawest and most disruptive, writes John Morrison in The Wire 512 Funkadelic Free Your Mind And Your Ass Will Follow Westbound/Org CD/DL/LP/2xLP/MC Maggot Brain Westbound/Org CD/DL/LP/2xLP/MC Released the summer before Jimi Hendrix tragically passed away in 1970, Free Your Mind And Your Ass Will Follow was not only the follow-up to Funkadelic’s promising self-titled debut of the same year; it signalled the arrival of George Clinton’s ensemble as the next great voice in Black rock and psychedelia. In his 2014 memoir Brothas Be, Yo Like George, Ain’t That Funkin’ Kinda Hard On You? , Clinton acknowledges this by contrasting the album’s release with Hendrix’s death, an event that he notes as “the end of an era”. During his brief but electrifying career, Hendrix moulded multiple aspects of the Black musical tradition – blues, rock, funk, R&B, jazz – into an electrifying corpus that opened up a new world of possibilities.

### From Relic to Infrastructure: Can India's Historic Water Systems Work Again?
Source: ArchDaily
Published: 2026-10-08 07:30 UTC
URL: https://www.archdaily.com/1186222/from-relic-to-infrastructure-can-indias-historic-water-systems-work-again
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

India's stepwells are usually encountered after their useful lives have ended. They are photographed, restored and interpreted as evidence of a water culture that once existed. Beneath the carved stone and descending stairs is a practical question: what happens when a structure built to manage water is put back to work? As Indian cities face water stress, seasonal rainfall and pressure on municipal systems, researchers and restoration efforts are examining whether traditional water structures can function again.

### When More Material Does Not Mean Better Performance
Source: ArchDaily
Published: 2026-10-08 06:45 UTC
URL: https://www.archdaily.com/1186502/when-more-material-does-not-mean-better-performance
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

In the story of the Three Little Pigs, the choice of material determines the fate of each house. The wolf's breath brings down the structures made of straw and wood, while the brick house remains standing. The moral is that the more solid the construction, the greater its ability to resist. A building, however, has to respond to much less straightforward questions. Remaining standing is still essential, but heat, light, air, and sound are not forces that necessarily need to be blocked. Sometimes sunlight needs to be kept out; at other times, daylight needs to be let in. Retaining heat may be desirable in winter and counterproductive in summer. Air needs to circulate, sound may need to be absorbed, and the conditions that determine these responses change throughout the day, across seasons, and over the life of the building itself. More material does not necessarily mean better performance. In some cases, it can simply add complexity without improving how the building responds.

### Volatiles takes the piece-by-piece map building of boardgames like Betrayal at House on the Hill and slaps a gory survival shooter on top of them
Source: Rock Paper Shotgun
Published: 2026-10-08 18:00 UTC
URL: https://www.rockpapershotgun.com/volatiles-takes-the-piece-by-piece-map-building-of-boardgames-like-betrayal-at-house-on-the-hill-and-slaps-a-gory-survival-shooter-on-top-of-them
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

I do love a boardgame where you reveal the tiles as you play. Sure, I also enjoy turning over all the pieces at the beginning of a game of Settlers of Catan and taking in all the tiles and trying to work out a strategy based on which resources are clustered near which port, but that's not the same as a game like Betrayal at House on the Hill, where you all start together in one single room and unveil the architecture of the house tile by tile as you explore. You have that same strategic eye, looking at the function and threat each room holds, but because the house is still being built, your plan can't become fixed. You have to reassess the layout of the building with each new piece and, of course, when you hit the moment in the game where one player betrays you the real danger arrives, all that planning now has to be twisted to oppose the new threat. Volatiles is an action game that plays with that same sense of strategy and mystery. It starts with you waking in a strange facility with little sense of what brought you there.

### Prospice: Anomalous Logistics is SnowRunner except instead of lengths of copper pipe, you're transporting the body parts of eldritch gods
Source: Rock Paper Shotgun
Published: 2026-10-08 17:30 UTC
URL: https://www.rockpapershotgun.com/prospice-anomalous-logistics-is-snowrunner-except-instead-of-lengths-of-copper-pipe-youre-transporting-the-body-parts-of-eldritch-gods
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Viewed from the front, my truck, with its winch and thick off-road tires, could be straight from SnowRunner central casting. Sure, it would an off-off-Broadway production of SnowRunner, with the polycount and texture resolution of a PS1-era take on the driving simulator, but I'm looking at a vehicle that is ready to struggle through thick mud and deep water; a petrol-guzzling workhorse. Then I walk around the side and glimpse the load it's hauling: a giant severed hand, freshly cut from the corpse of a buried god. Prospice: Anomalous Logistics has all the markings of SnowRunner, MudRunner and Spintires games, the driving simulators with a focus on overcoming difficult off-road terrain, but it's seen through the lens of eldritch horror and the new weird. Read more

### Lazare’s Sacrifice is a narrative deckbuilder made with Gwent's creator about avoiding the guillotine during the French Revolution
Source: Rock Paper Shotgun
Published: 2026-10-08 17:00 UTC
URL: https://www.rockpapershotgun.com/lazares-sacrifice-is-a-narrative-deckbuilder-made-with-gwents-creator-about-avoiding-the-guillotine-during-the-french-revolution
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

The sort of beautiful thing about triple-A games is that sometimes they have the resources to make an entirely new game within them. That was how Gwent was born, an in-world card game you can force Geralt into play that was eventually spun off into its own, fully-fledged thing. I imagine we'll be seeing a revamped version of it whenever The Witcher 4 arrives, but considering that's 2028 at the earliest, may I suggest making your bed with Lazare's Sacrifice, a narrative deckbuilder set during the French Revolution that's being made in collaboration with Gwent's lead designer Damien Monnier. Read more

### Introducing AI to an online petition platform changed outputs but not outcomes
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-08 20:27 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02580-8
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 30 September 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02580-8 Corpus et al. examine how the introduction of an AI writing tool impacted Change.org petitions, finding that it increased petition homogeneity but did not improve petition outcomes.

### Intrinsic rewards guide visual resource allocation via reinforcement learning
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-08 20:27 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02573-7
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 01 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02573-7 Tangible rewards are known to sharpen visual processing of associated objects. Tomić et al. show that an internal sense of confidence—acting as an intrinsic reward—similarly biases attention, revealing how the brain prioritizes limited resources.

### Selfish and prosocial individuals both distort their actions’ impact, but selfish distortions can be more contagious
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-08 20:27 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02585-3
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 01 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02585-3 In this study, Vollberg and Gross show that both selfish and prosocial individuals exaggerate the social benefits of their choices, with selfish-serving distortions proving to be socially contagious.

### Gendered motives for group formidability among children and adults
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-08 20:27 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02606-1
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 02 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02606-1 Fonn et al. find that boys but not girls prefer larger groups by age three, and these preferences predict support for group-based hierarchy among adults. This suggests that gendered motives for intergroup formidability are rooted in earliest childhood.

### Contract incentives and behaviours of medical consultants in Ireland
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-08 20:27 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02592-4
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 05 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02592-4 Using Irish inpatient data, this study finds that private practice within public hospitals, when paired with contractual ratio limits, can incentivize consultants to increase public admissions, but may reduce care quality for public patients.

### Algorithm-assisted personalized risk communication to encourage flu vaccination in the USA: three randomized field trials
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-08 20:27 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02603-4
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 05 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02603-4 Rosenbaum et al. show that targeted communications highlighting patients’ high-risk status increased influenza vaccination uptake. However, they did not find an additional effect of informing patients that a computer algorithm was used to identify their risk status.

### Study shows how children learn to talk about characters' thoughts and feelings in stories
Source: Phys.org
Published: 2026-10-08 20:20 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-10-children-characters-thoughts-stories.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

An international research effort has examined how children develop the ability to express what story characters think, feel and perceive. Research groups from around the world collected 5,507 picture-based stories from 2,763 typically developing monolingual and bilingual children ages 3 to 13. The children came from 32 countries and spoke 44 languages.

### Tracking down cellular gene functions with AI and microscopy
Source: Phys.org
Published: 2026-10-08 20:20 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-10-tracking-cellular-gene-functions-ai.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

A new technology promises to enable more comprehensive investigations into how individual genes determine the appearance and behavior of cells. Researchers led by professor Veit Hornung at LMU's Gene Center and professor Matthias Mann at the Max Planck Institute of Biochemistry in Martinsried, together with professor Fabian Theis at Helmholtz Munich, have developed SPARCS, a technology that combines artificial intelligence with microscopy and genetic screening.

### Rule gives every generation same share of its best attainable welfare
Source: Phys.org
Published: 2026-10-08 20:00 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-10-generation-welfare.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Sharing a finite resource across generations requires a balance between today's needs and the claims of those yet to be born. Research by Prof. Thomas A. Weber, presented at the 2026 European Control Conference in Reykjavík, proposes a fair allocation rule that gives every generation the same fraction of its best attainable welfare. The approach preserves access for all future generations and provides a performance guarantee without requiring agreement on a single social objective.

### Layered semiconductor unlocks magnetic control of light emitted by quantum condensates
Source: Phys.org
Published: 2026-10-08 20:00 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-10-layered-semiconductor-magnetic-emitted-quantum.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Quantum technologies are considered key technologies of the future. However, research in this field requires not only scientific expertise but sometimes also highly specialized laboratory conditions. Bose–Einstein condensates are a remarkable example, in which atoms move in lockstep, making quantum-mechanical effects visible not only at the level of individual atoms but also on a macroscopic scale. The first Bose–Einstein condensates were observed in ultracold atomic gases at temperatures close to absolute zero. The cooling required to achieve this was technically demanding, costly and restricted to specialized laboratories.

Light sheet microscopy allows biologists to selectively light up a thin slice of a sample. This method can improve image contrast by reducing background light while remaining gentle on the sample, reducing photodamage and photobleaching. Rice University's Anna-Karin Gustavsson has published a method in Nano Letters that expands the use of light sheet microscopy to work with a single objective in commercially available sample chambers.

New fossils from South Africa are reshaping the story of early human evolution. Summary: Researchers at the University of Oxford have discovered that human microglia can selectively prune away toxic alpha-synuclein aggregates from dopamine-producing neurons without destroying the host nerve cells. Utilizing co-cultures of human induced pluripotent stem cell (iPSC)-derived neurons and microglia, the team demonstrated that microglia employ “trogocytosis”, a precise cellular nibbling mechanism, regulated by GPNMB, P2RY12, and CD22 signaling pathways, along with an IL-10 molecular brake. The findings identify a distinct, neuroprotective microglial subtype that shields neurons in Parkinson’s disease. […]

### Effect of amaranth flatbread on maternal anthropometry during pregnancy: Evidence from a cluster-randomized controlled trial in Southern Ethiopia
Source: PLOS ONE Biology
Published: 2026-10-08 20:27 UTC
URL: https://journals.plos.org/plosone/article?id=10.1371/journal.pone.0360480
Source meta: journal · primary · R4 · P4 · signal

by Amanuel Yoseph, Lakew Mussie, Mehretu Belayneh, Francisco Guillen-Grima Introduction Maternal undernutrition and iron deficiency anemia (IDA) remain major public health challenges in Ethiopia, contributing to adverse pregnancy outcomes and poor maternal health. Amaranth, a nutrient-dense pseudocereal, has demonstrated potential to improve maternal nutritional status, yet evidence from rigorous trials is limited. To evaluate the effect of amaranth grain flatbread on maternal anthropometric indicators - mid-upper arm circumference (MUAC), body mass index (BMI), and gestational weight change (GWG) – as proxies for overall nutritional status among pregnant women in Hawela Lida district, Southern Ethiopia. Methods A parallel-group, two-arm cluster randomized controlled trial was conducted from September 2024 to February 2025.

### Eastern origin and three-millennia persistence of a founding grapevine lineage in Iberian viticulture
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-08 20:27 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01090-0?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Pérez-Jordà et al. use archaeogenomics to show that eastern domesticated grapevines laid the foundations for Iberian viticulture 3,000 years ago. Genetic relatedness indicates these introductions gave rise to extant Iberian cultivars through a few sexual generations, revealing clonal propagation of the cultivar Heben from the 10th century to the present.

### Ketamine attenuates habenula activity in response to aversive outcomes during Pavlovian learning
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-08 20:27 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01095-X?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Translating a preclinical model to humans using 7-tesla MRI, Pulcu et al. show that ketamine reduces habenula activity for the expectation of aversive outcomes during Pavlovian learning. This provides direct translational evidence for how ketamine modulates the neural circuitry implicated in depression, relevant to its rapid antidepressant effects.

### Cambrian fossils illuminate the ambulacrarian common ancestor
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-08 20:27 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01104-8?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

He et al. describe Yujingia, a new early Cambrian ambulacrarian with differentiated appendages that include pinnate arms with filter-feeding grooves. The morphology and phylogenetic position of Yujingia suggest that modern ambulacrarians, including echinoderms and hemichordates, evolved from a tentaculate, semi-sessile suspension-feeding ancestor.

### Quantifying the control laws governing terminal attack in lions
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-08 20:27 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01196-6?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Duporge and Frost combine drone videography with AI-based pose estimation to reconstruct the kinematics of 67 lion (Panthera leo) attacks. Speed is adjusted to regulate lateral acceleration during turns, while lion steering is guided by proportional navigation and proportional pursuit, a mixed guidance law previously identified only in aerial predators.

### Thirst reweights temperature preference through dual dopaminergic and endocrine modulation in Drosophila
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-08 20:27 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01114-0?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Chiang et al. show that thirst shifts Drosophila toward cooler temperatures by bidirectionally reweighting cold and hot responses in the mushroom body. Dopamine- and 20E-mediated pathways differentially tune these responses, which converge on a common avoidance-promoting output.

### Spatial proteomics reveals carboxysome adaptation and mitochondrial proteome in Paulinella chromatophora
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-08 20:27 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01113-9?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Using LOPIT-DC-based spatial proteomics, Cadena et al. map the sub-organellar protein landscape of Paulinella chromatophora, a cercozoan with evolutionary-early-stage photosynthetic organelles. They find nucleus-encoded carboxysome-associated proteins potentially forming a novel O2-scavenging system that may enhance carbon fixation efficiency. The mitochondrial sub-proteome reveals conserved membrane organization alongside divergent, lineage-specific proteins.

Functional profiling of ∼200,000 genomic segments from vertebrate-infecting viruses uncovers RNA elements that boost gene expression by stabilizing mRNAs and enhancing translation, revealing the diversity of TENT4-dependent mixed tailing and identifying canonical poly(A) polymerase-mediated cytoplasmic polyadenylation, together expanding our understanding of post-transcriptional regulation and RNA therapeutic design.

### [Series] Peripheral arterial disease: adverse impacts, disparities in outcomes, and the path ahead
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-10-08 20:27 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)00847-0/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

This is the third paper in the peripheral arterial disease (PAD) Series. The first and second papers in this Series outline that PAD affects over 300 million people worldwide and can be diagnosed by non-invasive tests, as well as that treatment requires medications to reduce modifiable risk factors and that exercise therapy and revascularisation are required in selected patients. In this paper, the devastating effects of PAD at the individual level are described, including chronic pain, functional limitation, loss of independence, impaired physical and mental quality of life, unemployment, and financial instability.

### [Series] Current advancements in the care of peripheral arterial disease: modern approaches to medical, endovascular, and surgical treatment
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-10-08 20:27 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)00845-7/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

People with peripheral arterial disease (PAD) of the lower limbs are at very high risk of cardiovascular events and therefore the cornerstone of their management is medical therapy and behaviour modification to reduce that risk. The risk of limb loss (through major amputation) is low in people with asymptomatic PAD or intermittent claudication with only a few patients progressing to the most severe form of PAD: chronic limb threatening ischaemia. Supervised exercise therapy is effective in patients with intermittent claudication to improve their walking capacity and avoids the risks associated with revascularisation procedures to improve blood flow to the limb.

### [Series] Understanding peripheral arterial disease in the current era
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-10-08 20:27 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)00846-9/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease is the primary driver of premature death in global populations in recent decades. Mortality statistics, most publications, and guidelines in the atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease field have traditionally focused on cardiac and cerebrovascular adverse events and causes of death. However, given the increasing prevalence of lower extremity peripheral arterial disease and its expanding burden on global health systems, such an emphasis might represent a skewed perception of vascular risk within the broader atherosclerotic spectrum.

### Study of the dark energy models in f(Q, Lm) gravity in different formalisms
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-08 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.08824
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.08824v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: We study the late time cosmological evolution of the Universe in the framework of a flat Friedmann Lemaitre Robertson-Walker spacetime in f(Q,Lm) gravity. The cosmic dynamics of the Friedmann equations are discussed using various equation of state (EoS) parameters like Chevallier Polarski Linder (CPL), Jassal Bagla Padmanabhan (JBP), Barboza Alcaniz (BA), and Logarithmic (LOG). We use joint observational datasets of Hubble (OHD), DES five year Type Ia supernovae (DES 5YR), and the Baryon Acoustic Oscillation (BAO) measurements to obtain the best fit values of model parameters, which show good agreement with current observations. These models exhibit a smooth transition from early deceleration to late time acceleration. The equation of state parameter displays quintessence like behaviour over a wide redshift range and gradually converges its behaviour from quintessence-like to phantom-like behaviour as z tends to -1, except in the BA case.

### Gravity, Entropy, and the Second Law: A Pedagogical Perspective
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-08 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.08832
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.08832v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: An interesting question for discussion in university physics courses is whether gravity can conflict with the second law of thermodynamics. In this work, we address this question by comparing the behavior of an ideal gas in two conceptually different regimes: one in which gravitational effects can be neglected and another in which self-gravity becomes important. In the first case, free expansion provides the familiar example in which an increase in the volume accessible to the gas leads to an increase in entropy. Self-gravitating systems behave differently: gravitational contraction can reduce the volume occupied by matter while releasing energy and increasing the entropy of the surroundings. This illustrates why the usual intuition that increasing entropy necessarily corresponds to increasing spatial dispersion cannot be applied directly to self-gravitating systems.

arXiv:2610.08836v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: One of the key unresolved questions in modern cosmology is the Hubble tension, which arises from the inconsistency between determinations of the Hubble constant ($H_0$) based on nearby observations and those predicted by early-Universe data under the standard $\Lambda$CDM paradigm. Recent advances have identified the new Dark Energy Spectroscopic Instrument (DESI) survey as a promising observational tool for probing late-time cosmology. In this paper, we use the latest DESI DR2 baryon acoustic oscillation (BAO) measurements, combined with nonparametrically reconstructed Type Ia supernovae and cosmic chronometer data, to constrain the Hubble constant using three complementary machine learning methodologies: Gaussian process regression (GPR), artificial neural networks (ANNs), and long short-term memory (LSTM) networks.
